Pharma: pipeline readouts, guidance raise
LLY · fiscal Q2 2026 call, reported 2026-08-05 · 119 transcript paragraphs

Claude Opus 5.59.0

Eli Lilly grew Q2 2026 revenue 48% year over year, with MOUNJARO and ZEPBOUND at $14.9 billion, up $6.3 billion, and non-GAAP performance margin at 54.8%. Lucas Montarce, CFO, raised full-year revenue guidance to $85–87 billion. Much of the Q&A covered the Foundayo launch, the Medicare Bridge program and the retatrutide BLA pathway.

Key takeaways

  • Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA, said Foundayo inflected in the last week of July, with volume almost doubling versus a month earlier and about 1 in 4 new starts on Foundayo.
  • Foundayo prescribers rose from 8,000 at the last earnings call to 36,000.
  • Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO, said Lilly views retatrutide as a biologic, but the first court decision was mixed and the US submission is now expected in Q1 2027.
  • Ilya Yuffa said the Medicare Bridge program extends access to about 35% more Americans, with about 80% of Bridge patients on injectables and 60%–70% new to treatment.
  • US price fell 3%, or 9% excluding rebate and discount estimate changes, which Lucas Montarce, CFO, does not expect to recur in H2.

Key metrics

  • Revenue growth 48% vs Q2 2025
  • MOUNJARO + ZEPBOUND revenue $14.9B +$6.3B YoY
  • Non-GAAP performance margin 54.8% +9 pts YoY
  • Non-GAAP EPS $8.38 $6.31 in Q2 2025
  • Gross margin 86.3% +~1.3 pts YoY
  • US price -3% (-9% ex adjustments) YoY
  • US obesity incretin TRx growth 78% YoY; Lilly ~6/10 TRx
  • Foundayo prescribers 36,000 8,000 at last earnings

Financial highlights

  • Revenue grew 48% year over year, and key products added almost $6.8 billion.
  • Gross margin was 86.3%, up about 1.3 points, and non-GAAP performance margin was 54.8%, up 9 points.
  • Non-GAAP EPS was $8.38 including a $3.03 acquired IPR&D charge, versus $6.31 in Q2 2025.
  • By region, US revenue grew 33%, Europe 55% in constant currency (including a $250M Jardiance milestone), China 93% and rest of world 136%.
  • Lilly paid $1.5B in dividends and repurchased $1.6B of shares.

Guidance

  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, raised FY2026 revenue guidance to $85–87B from $82–85B.
  • He raised FY2026 non-GAAP performance margin guidance to 49%–50.5% from 47%–48.5%.
  • He guided FY2026 non-GAAP EPS to $35.50–36.50, which he said reflects a $2.78 midpoint increase before the $3.03 Q2 IPR&D charge (prior range $35.50–37).
  • Dan Skovronsky, Chief Scientific and Product Officer, expects the retatrutide US submission in Q1 2027; Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO, expects a Foundayo Type 2 diabetes US regulatory action later this year.
  • Lucas Montarce expects US obesity net price to decline, with the decline embedded in guidance and more than offset by volume, and expects Foundayo to roll out in major markets in 2027.

Q&A

  • Asad Haider, Goldman Sachs: He asked why the US Foundayo launch had been slower than expected, and how Lilly competes outside the US given Novo's claimed 50% share in the UAE.
    Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA and Global Customer Capabilities: Yuffa said CVS added Foundayo in June, Bridge launched July 1 and full DTC began, with an inflection in the last week of July, volume almost doubling and about 1 in 4 new starts on Foundayo. Patrik Jonsson said Lilly's combined incretin share in the UAE held steady and that competitor data reflects inventory build, with most international launches in 2027.
    Answered partially, disputing the premise of the competitor's share figure.
  • Courtney Breen, Bernstein: She asked what is required for retatrutide to be accepted as a BLA, and whether future incretins could also qualify.
    Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO: Ricks said Lilly views retatrutide as a biologic by amino acid count, that the first court decision was mixed and litigation is active, and that more CMC data is needed before a submission expected by Q1 next year.
    Answered partially; he did not address other incretins.
  • Akash Tewari, Jefferies: He asked whether the H2 revenue deceleration implied by guidance reflects price or conservatism.
    Lucas Montarce, Chief Financial Officer: Montarce said the guidance midpoint rose $2.5B, that rebate estimate adjustments will not recur in H2, that H2 2025 included a bolus of MOUNJARO country launches, and that seasonality affects Europe in Q3 and US Type 2 diabetes in Q4.
    Answered directly, disputing the premise.
  • Chris Schott, JPMorgan: He asked for early learnings from the Medicare Bridge rollout and the mix of orals versus injectables.
    Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA and Global Customer Capabilities: Yuffa said Bridge covers about 35% more Americans, ZEPBOUND and MOUNJARO have inflected in recent weeks, about 80% of Bridge patients are on injectables, and 60%–70% are new patients.
    Answered directly.
  • Umer Raffat, Evercore: He asked how sustainable obesity net price of about $580 per script is, given cash prices and medical exceptions.
    Lucas Montarce, Chief Financial Officer: Montarce cited LillyDirect prices of $299–449 and the Bridge price of $245, said CVS access returns starting in Q4 and medical exceptions are short-term, and said price is expected to fall, which is embedded in guidance.
    Answered partially; he did not quantify the rebate impact.
  • Terence Flynn, Morgan Stanley: He asked how extensive Foundayo sampling is, whether it dampens IQVIA data, and whether the prescriber base is expanding.
    Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA and Global Customer Capabilities: Yuffa said samples cover the first 30 days of the first dose and are used extensively, and prescribers grew from 8,000 to 36,000.
    Declined to quantify the IQVIA effect.

Unanswered

  • Courtney Breen, Bernstein: Whether future incretins could also qualify as biologics under a BLA. Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO, did not address this part of the question.
  • Terence Flynn, Morgan Stanley: How much Foundayo sampling dampens IQVIA prescription data. Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA, described sampling but did not quantify its effect.
  • Jason Gerberry, Bank of America: Whether psychiatry and vaccines are scalable priority areas or one-off deals. Jake Van Naarden, President of Lilly Oncology, declined to look forward and said Lilly will be opportunistic.

Notable quotes

  • “While we're pleased with the progress this year so far, we're not satisfied.” Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO
  • “We're starting to see an inflection point on Foundayo now.” Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA
  • “In general, we need to have in mind that this is very early data, and there is a lot of noise in the data set.” Patrik Jonsson, President of Lilly International
  • “There was a mixed decision in the first decision in the courts. Obviously, it's active litigation.” Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO
  • “The medical exception is maybe a short-term thing and our all along view is to have open access for all the patients.” Lucas Montarce, CFO

What changed

  • Guidance: FY2026 revenue: $82–85B to $85–87B.
  • Guidance: Non-GAAP performance margin: 47%–48.5% to 49%–50.5%.
  • Guidance: Non-GAAP EPS: $35.50–37 to $35.50–36.50, after absorbing the $3.03 Q2 IPR&D charge.
  • Guidance: Foundayo international rollout: 40 countries under review with launches exiting this year into next, to 40+ markets under review and major-market rollout in 2027.
  • New mentions: Onswik (insulin efsitora alfa), AtaiBeckley / BPL-003, Curevo / amezosvatein, LimmaTech Biologics, The Vaccine Company (EBV), Tersolisib, Brenipatide in IBS, VERVE-102
  • Dropped: Lilly Employer Connect and commercial employer coverage, Obesity market segmentation with eloralintide, Incretin price elasticity and margin scenarios
  • Tone: Discussion of Foundayo moved from Ilya Yuffa's "First it is early days" in Q1 to "We're starting to see an inflection point on Foundayo now." Retatrutide discussion shifted from data readouts to regulatory and litigation hedging.

Risks mentioned

  • Retatrutide BLA pathway is in active litigation after a mixed first court decision, and more CMC data is needed before filing.
  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, expects US obesity net price to decline as medical exceptions fall away.
  • Generic semaglutide has entered India, Brazil and Canada, though Patrik Jonsson, President of Lilly International, said generic makers face supply constraints.
  • Lucas Montarce cited H2 seasonality from European Q3 vacations and the US Type 2 diabetes market in Q4.
  • Retatrutide is available from illegitimate sources ahead of approval.
Claude Opus 5.5 · 9.0

Accurate, well-attributed and tighter than E, with fair partial-answer reads, a strong unanswered list and a genuine Foundayo rollout-timing change; the only weak spot is the "declined" label on the IQVIA point, which was simply skipped.

  • Marketing, selling and administrative expenses rose 25% and R&D expenses rose 14% versus Q2 2025.

Guidance

  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, guides full-year 2026 revenue to $85 billion to $87 billion; prior was $82 billion to $85 billion.
  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, guides full-year 2026 non-GAAP performance margin to 49% to 50.5%; prior was 47% to 48.5%.
  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, guides full-year 2026 non-GAAP EPS to $35.50 to $36.50, before updating for the $3.03 Q2 acquired IPR&D charge; prior was $35.50 to $37.
  • Dan Skovronsky, CSPO, expects the retatrutide U.S. BLA submission in Q1 2027.
  • Dave Ricks, CEO, expects Foundayo Type 2 diabetes U.S. regulatory action later this year and says Medicare Bridge eligibility expands to 20 million eligible Americans from July 1, 2026.

Q&A

  • Asad Haider, Goldman Sachs: Reasons for the slower-than-anticipated U.S. Foundayo launch curve and when an inflection is expected.
    Ilya Yuffa, President, Lilly USA and Global Customer Capabilities: Yuffa cited physician education, access and affordability, and consumer awareness as focus areas; CVS added Foundayo in June. The last week of July showed inflection with volume almost doubling versus a month earlier, and around one in four new starts are on Foundayo.
    partial
  • Courtney Breen, Bernstein: Requirements and milestones to get retatrutide accepted as a BLA.
    Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO: Lilly has all clinical data needed but needs more CMC work, is in communication with the FDA, and will update on filing status. Submission is targeted for Q1 next year, and the court decision on biologic status is mixed with active litigation.
    partial
  • Akash Tewari, Jefferies: Whether guidance implies a second-half revenue deceleration versus Q2.
    Lucas Montarce, Chief Financial Officer: Pre-period U.S. rebate and discount estimate adjustments this quarter will not continue into the second half.     partial
  • Umer Raffat, Evercore: Sustainability of net pricing on obesity given medical exceptions and the CVS formulary return.
    Lucas Montarce, Chief Financial Officer: ZEPBOUND prices range from $299 to $449 on LillyDirect and $245 under Bridge. Medical exceptions are a short-term driver, the CVS access agreement starts in Q4, and price decline is embedded in guidance and more than offset by volume growth.
    partial
  • Jason Gerberry, Bank of America: Whether psychiatry and vaccines are scalable priority areas or one-off deals.
    Jake Van Naarden, President, Lilly Oncology and Head of Business Development: Van Naarden said both areas share immense unmet need and the deals offer impactful technology at disciplined prices. He declined to forward-look on further infectious disease or psychiatry deals, saying Lilly will be opportunistic.
    declined to quantify

Patrik Jonsson, President, Lilly International, said Lilly is probably slightly better positioned with an oral but reimbursement takes time and launch focus is chronic weight management, without a timing or figure.

Gemini 3.8 Flash7.0

Eli Lilly delivered 48% worldwide revenue growth year-over-year in Q2 2026, driven by $14.9 billion in combined Mounjaro and Zepbound revenue. The company raised full-year 2026 revenue guidance by $2.5 billion at the midpoint and increased performance margin projections. U.S. demand accelerated with early adoption of the Medicare GLP-1 Bridge program and an inflection in the oral incretin Foundayo.

Key takeaways

  • Combined global sales of Mounjaro and Zepbound reached $14.9 billion, an increase of $6.3 billion compared to Q2 2025.
  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, raised full-year 2026 revenue guidance to $85.0 billion to $87.0 billion and increased performance margin guidance to 49.0% to 50.5%.
  • U.S. Foundayo volume inflected in late July, nearly doubling month-over-month, while active U.S. prescribers grew from 8,000 to 36,000.
  • The newly launched Medicare GLP-1 Bridge program expanded eligible access by roughly 35%, with 60% to 70% of participants new to treatment.
  • Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO, confirmed Lilly is targeting a Q1 2027 submission for retatrutide under a BLA regulatory pathway.

Key metrics

  • Q2 2026 Worldwide Revenue Growth 48% vs. Q2 2025
  • Combined Mounjaro and Zepbound Revenue $14.9B up $6.3B vs. Q2 2025
  • Non-GAAP Diluted EPS $8.38 vs. $6.31 in Q2 2025
  • Gross Margin 86.3% up ~1.3 percentage points vs. Q2 2025
  • Non-GAAP Performance Margin 54.8% up 9 percentage points vs. Q2 2025
  • U.S. Revenue Growth 33% vs. Q2 2025
  • Oncology, Immunology, Neuroscience Portfolio Growth 121% vs. Q2 2025
  • Share Repurchases $1.6B in Q2 2026

Financial highlights

  • Worldwide revenue grew 48% year-over-year in Q2 2026, with key products contributing an additional $6.8 billion.
  • Non-GAAP diluted EPS reached $8.38 compared to $6.31 in Q2 2025, which included $3.03 of acquired IPR&D charges.
  • Gross margin expanded by approximately 1.3 percentage points year-over-year to 86.3%.
  • Non-GAAP performance margin rose 9 percentage points over Q2 2025 to 54.8%.
  • Capital return included $1.5 billion in cash dividends paid and $1.6 billion deployed into share repurchases.

Guidance

  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, raised FY 2026 revenue guidance to $85.0 billion to $87.0 billion, up from $82.0 billion to $85.0 billion.
  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, increased FY 2026 non-GAAP performance margin guidance to 49.0% to 50.5%, up from 47.0% to 48.5%.
  • Lucas Montarce, CFO, updated FY 2026 non-GAAP EPS guidance to $35.50 to $36.50, compared to the prior range of $35.50 to $37.00.

Q&A

  • Seamus Fernandez, Guggenheim: How does Lilly envision expanding the obesity consumer treatment opportunity going forward, and how does the U.S. self-pay market compare to international markets?
    Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA and Global Customer Capabilities: Obesity utilization remains in the mid-single digits globally, leaving substantial runway through LillyDirect, direct-to-consumer advertising, the Bridge program, and state Medicaid, with approximately 55% of Zepbound prescriptions currently in self-pay.
    answered directly
  • Courtney Breen, Bernstein: What are the requirements and milestones ahead to ensure retatrutide is accepted via the BLA regulatory pathway, and could subsequent incretins also be classified as BLAs?
    Dave Ricks, Chair and CEO: Lilly maintains retatrutide qualifies as a BLA based on amino acid count and protein analogy despite an adverse initial court ruling, with all clinical data gathered, CMC finishing, and ongoing FDA talks toward a planned Q1 2027 submission.
    answered partially
  • Akash Tewari, Jefferies: Does guidance imply revenue deceleration in the second half of 2026, and is that due to pricing assumptions or conservatism?
    Lucas Montarce, Chief Financial Officer: The guidance accounts for the non-recurrence of favorable H1 gross-to-net true-ups, tough 2025 comps from ex-U.S. Mounjaro launches, Q3 European summer vacation seasonality, and Q4 U.S. diabetes seasonality, while absolute dollar growth remains substantial.
    answered directly
  • Terence Flynn, Morgan Stanley: How extensive is the Foundayo sampling program, and how significantly has the U.S. prescriber base expanded?
    Ilya Yuffa, President of Lilly USA and Global Customer Capabilities: Sampling comprises standard 30-day first doses backed by broad inventory, while active prescribers increased to 36,000 from 8,000 in the prior quarter.
    answered directly
  • Umer Raffat, Evercore: How sustainable is obesity net pricing given medical exception dynamics, and will adding CVS to formulary reduce realized price per pack?
    Lucas Montarce, Chief Financial Officer: Realized price per pack reflects varied channels and script sizes, and expanded formulary access like CVS in Q4 will reduce the proportion of high-priced medical exceptions, which is already budgeted in guidance and offset by volume.
    answered directly
  • Jason Gerberry, Bank of America: Is the expansion into psychiatry and vaccines a strategic priority to scale, or were those acquisitions opportunistic, one-off scientific transactions?
    Jake Van Naarden, President of Lilly Oncology and Head of Business Development: Both spaces represent areas of massive unmet need where Lilly pursued scientific opportunities at disciplined valuations, and Lilly plans to remain opportunistic rather than pursue a predefined scale target.
    answered directly

Unanswered

Dave Ricks reiterated Lilly's stance on amino acid length and an ongoing lawsuit, but did not address whether subsequent pipeline incretins would be submitted as BLAs.

  • “people will vote with their feet and embrace the really guaranteed product.” Ken Custer, President of Lilly Cardiometabolic Health

Risks mentioned

Restaurant: comps, traffic, unit growth
Chipotle Mexican Grill, Inc. · fiscal Q2 2026 call, reported 2026-07-29 · 121 transcript paragraphs

Claude Sonnet 5.58.0

Chipotle's Q2 2026 revenue rose 9.3% to $3.3B on comparable restaurant sales up 2.2%, with transactions up 1%. Restaurant level margin was 25.2%, down 220bp YoY, and adjusted diluted EPS was $0.33, flat YoY. Adam Rymer, CFO, guided Q3 comps to around +1%, assuming a roughly 200bp Cyclospora-related softening continues.

Key takeaways

  • Comparable sales rose 2.2%, with transactions up 1%, against +0.5% comp and about +60bps transactions in Q1.
  • Adam Rymer, CFO, said trends softened about 200bp in the second half of July around the Cyclospora industry issue, and Q3 is the toughest lap.
  • Scott Boatwright, CEO, said HEAP will reach about 2,000 restaurants by year-end and the full portfolio sometime in 2027.
  • Digital sales were $1.3B, 38.3% of sales versus 35.5% a year earlier, with 23 million active rewards members.
  • Boatwright said a one-stop pay loyalty pilot starts in August and described the current in-restaurant experience as clunky.

Key metrics

  • Revenue $3.3B, +9.3%
  • Comparable restaurant sales +2.2% transaction comp +1%
  • Digital sales $1.3B, 38.3% of sales 35.5% prior year
  • Restaurant level margin 25.2% down 220bp YoY
  • Adjusted diluted EPS $0.33 flat YoY
  • New restaurants 101 80 Chipotlanes, 1 international partner-operated
  • Pricing impact ~1.6%
  • Rewards members 23 million active

Financial highlights

  • Revenue was $3.3B, up 9.3%, with pricing impact of about 1.6%.
  • Cost of sales was 29.7% (up ~80bp), labor 25% (up ~30bp) and other operating costs 14.9% (up ~90bp).
  • Marketing was 3% of sales, up ~30bp; non-GAAP G&A was $176M versus GAAP $190M.
  • Chipotle repurchased $631M of stock in Q2 at a $32.55 average, over $1.3B year to date at $34.35, with $1.7B remaining.
  • Cash and investments were $800M, down $1.3B YoY.

Guidance

  • Adam Rymer, CFO, expects full-year 2026 comps in the low single-digit range and Q3 comps around +1%, assuming the ~200bp Cyclospora impact continues.
  • Rymer expects Q3 pricing in the mid 2% range and full-year pricing near the high end of 1%-2%.
  • Rymer guided Q3 cost of sales just under 30%, labor in the mid 25% range, other operating costs in the mid 15% range, and marketing in the low 3% range.
  • Rymer guided Q3 non-GAAP G&A around $180M, full-year depreciation around 3% of sales and underlying tax rate 24%-26%.
  • Scott Boatwright, CEO, expects about 350 company openings, about 80% with a Chipotlane, in 2026.

Q&A

  • Dennis Geiger, UBS: What is the color on the low single-digit comp guide, Q3 and recent softening?
    Adam Rymer, CFO: Traffic improved through Q2 and carried into early July, then softened about 200bp in the second half of July around Cyclospora. Q3 comp is about +1% if that impact continues. Boatwright said Chipotle is hit on sales but not involved, as the products are not on the menu and its lettuce is from California.
    Answered directly.
  • Brian Vaccaro, Raymond James: Does the 1% Q3 guide imply a ~3% underlying trend with a 2% headwind, and where is Max 15 throughput?
    Adam Rymer, CFO: Yes, the guide assumes the impact lasts through the quarter, though duration is hard to predict. HEAP restaurants do two or three more entrees in Max 15. In-store is in the low 20s, digital (almost 40% of sales) mid-teens, combined low to mid-30s per restaurant per day.
    Answered directly.
  • Chris Carril, KeyBanc: What is the margin outlook beyond Q3 and the gap between pricing and inflation?
    Adam Rymer, CFO: The gap was widest in 1H, with pricing around 1% and inflation low to mid 3%. In Q3 inflation is about 3% and pricing mid 2%. From Q4, Rymer expects them to match.
    Answered directly.
  • Drew North, Baird: Was there World Cup regional divergence, and why trend toward the high end of 1-2% pricing?
    Adam Rymer, CFO: World Cup impact was under 5bp for the quarter; about 300 restaurants within 10 miles of stadiums saw a 50-100bp comp benefit. Rolling pricing measures resistance and will continue; the earlier gap was timing.
    Answered directly.
  • Danilo Gargiulo, Bernstein: Can you size the capex and sales uplift from refreshing the store base?
    Scott Boatwright, CEO: Remodel refresh is being stage-gated at several locations at $100,000-$300,000 per restaurant, with nothing to report yet on uplift. No aesthetic remodel has been done in the brand's history.
    Declined to quantify uplift.
  • David Palmer, Evercore ISI: Why be confident momentum picks up into 2027 given tough comparisons and the slowdown?
    Scott Boatwright, CEO: He cited early proof points from HEAP, throughput and hospitality, plus menu innovation, rewards, brand communication and group occasions, building through 2H26 and into 2027.
    Answered partially, no figures.

Unanswered

  • David Palmer, Evercore ISI: Why be confident momentum picks up into 2027 given tough comparisons and recent slowdown? Boatwright pointed to green shoots and early proof points without a figure or timing for the comp improvement.
  • Danilo Gargiulo, Bernstein: What is the incremental capex and sales uplift from refreshing the store base? Boatwright gave only a $100,000-$300,000 per-restaurant range and said he had nothing to report on uplift.
  • Peter Saleh, BTIG: Are the new menu items center-of-the-plate or sides? Boatwright said work spans center-of-plate, beverage, sides and desserts, with some ideas possibly in 2H26 and certainly 2027, without specifics.
  • Jim Salera, Stephens: How much do LTOs contribute to frequency uplift? Rymer gave no LTO-specific figures, only 'hundreds of basis points' of transaction lift typically.

Notable quotes

  • “Very difficult to predict how long this could last.” Adam Rymer, CFO
  • “It's really a clunky experience.” Scott Boatwright, CEO
  • “I have nothing to report today, unfortunately, but I'll tell you that work is in flight.” Scott Boatwright, CEO

What changed

  • Guidance: Full-year comps: about flat to low single-digit range.
  • Guidance: Q3 comp guide is around +1%; prior Q2 comp guide was around +1%.
  • Guidance: Full-year pricing: 1%-2% to near the high end of 1%-2%, with Q3 pricing in the mid 2% range versus about 1.5% in Q2.
  • Guidance: Q3 non-GAAP G&A guide is around $180M versus about $181M for Q2.
  • Guidance: Marketing: below 3% of sales to low 3% range.
  • New mentions: Cyclospora, HEAP, Linebacker and Expo, Matchday BOGO, Monterrey, Mexico (Alsea), Seoul and Singapore (SPC), Saudi Arabia, Remodel refresh program
  • Dropped: Delivery premium and aggregators, Happier Hour test, Zipline drone delivery, Hourly turnover
  • Tone: Prior-quarter language stressed a cautious outlook given the dynamic consumer environment; this call centers on a specific Cyclospora-related ~200bp softening, while Scott Boatwright, CEO, points to green shoots.

Risks mentioned

  • Adam Rymer, CFO, said the Cyclospora industry issue cut trends about 200bp in late July and its duration is very difficult to predict.
  • Q3 is the toughest comparison lap, per Rymer.
  • Inflation was low to mid 3% in 1H versus pricing around 1%, pressuring margins.
  • Boatwright said opening more than 350 restaurants a year risks diminishing returns or fracturing the base.
  • Boatwright said LTOs reach a point of diminishing returns, maybe the fifth or sixth time.

Claude Fable 5.18.0

Chipotle reported Q2 2026 revenue of $3.3B, up 9.3% year-over-year, with comparable restaurant sales up 2.2% (transactions +1%, pricing ~1.6%) and adjusted diluted EPS of $0.33, flat to last year, while restaurant-level margin fell 220 bps to 25.2%. Adam Rymer, CFO, raised full-year comp guidance to low single digits from about flat but guided Q3 comps to around +1%, embedding a roughly 200 bps industry Cyclospora headwind that began in the second half of July. Scott Boatwright, CEO, framed the quarter as early days of the Recipe for Growth strategy, pointing to menu innovation, the HEAP rollout and the rewards relaunch.

Key takeaways

  • Adam Rymer, CFO, said traffic improved through Q2 and into early July, then comps softened about 200 bps in the second half of July around the industry Cyclospora issue; Scott Boatwright, CEO, said Chipotle is not involved and the implicated products are not on its menu.
  • Rymer confirmed the Q3 guide of ~1% implies an underlying ~3% trend with the 200 bps headwind assumed to persist, and called Q3 the toughest lap of the year.
  • Boatwright said HEAP is in more than 1,000 restaurants, yields two to four labor hours reinvested in prep and peak line, and adds two to three entrees in Max 15, with 2,000 targeted by year-end and the full portfolio in 2027.
  • Digital sales reached $1.3B, 38.3% of sales versus 35.5% a year ago; Boatwright attributed the ~18% growth to the rewards relaunch and Summer of Extras, with a frictionless in-restaurant rewards pay pilot launching in August.
  • Rymer said younger and lower-income cohorts, previously most pressured, improved the most, and Boatwright cited the highest year-over-year wallet share gains since 2024 across all income and age groups.

Key metrics

  • Revenue $3.3B +9.3% y/y
  • Comparable restaurant sales +2.2% transactions +1%, pricing ~1.6%
  • Digital sales $1.3B, 38.3% of sales 35.5% prior year
  • Restaurant-level margin 25.2% down 220 bps y/y
  • Adjusted diluted EPS $0.33 flat y/y
  • New restaurants opened 101 80 Chipotlanes
  • HEAP installed 1,000+ restaurants 2-3 extra entrees in peak 15 minutes
  • Share repurchases $631M in Q2 at $32.55 $1.7B authorization remaining

Financial highlights

  • Revenue $3.3B, up 9.3% year-over-year; comps +2.2% with transaction comp +1% and pricing ~1.6%.
  • Restaurant-level margin 25.2%, down 220 bps, with cost of sales 29.7% (+~80 bps), labor 25% (+~30 bps), other operating 14.9% (+~90 bps) and marketing 3% (+~30 bps).
  • Adjusted diluted EPS $0.33, flat year-over-year; non-GAAP G&A $176M ($148M underlying).
  • Opened 101 restaurants (100 company-owned, 80 Chipotlanes, 1 international partner-operated); new unit productivity ~80% with ~60% year-two cash-on-cash returns.
  • Repurchased $631M in Q2 at $32.55 average and $1.3B+ year-to-date; $1.7B authorization remaining after a $1.3B addition, with cash and investments of $800M, down $1.3B year-over-year.

Guidance

  • Adam Rymer, CFO, raised FY2026 comparable sales to low single-digit range from about flat; Q3 comps around +1% assuming the ~200 bps Cyclospora impact persists.
  • Rymer expects Q3 pricing in the mid 2% range against inflation of about 3%, with pricing and inflation expected to match from Q4 forward; full-year pricing near the high end of 1%-2%.
  • Q3 cost lines per Rymer: cost of sales just under 30%, labor mid 25%, other operating mid 15%, marketing low 3%, non-GAAP G&A around $180M; FY depreciation ~3% of sales and tax rate 24%-26%.
  • Scott Boatwright, CEO, reiterated approximately 350 openings in 2026, about 80% with Chipotlanes, and HEAP in ~2,000 restaurants by year-end with the full portfolio sometime in 2027.
  • Boatwright expects Seoul in 2026, Singapore early 2027, Mexico City 2027, Saudi Arabia in the near future, and a national catering/Build-Your-Own Chipotle launch in 2027.

Q&A

  • Dennis Geiger, UBS: Color on the low single-digit comp guide, Q3 and recent softening trends.
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: Traffic improved through Q2 and into the first half of July; second-half July comps softened ~200 bps around the industry Cyclospora issue. Q3 comps expected around +1% assuming the impact continues, as duration is hard to predict; Q3 is the toughest lap of the year. Scott Boatwright added Chipotle is not involved and sources lettuce in California.
    Answered directly
  • Brian Vaccaro, Raymond James: Does the ~1% Q3 guide imply an underlying ~3% trend with a 200 bps headwind persisting, and where is Max 15 throughput ex-HEAP?
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: Yes; expense guidance assumes the impact continues through the quarter. HEAP restaurants do two or three more entrees in Max 15; in-store Max 15 is in the low 20s, digital (almost 40% of sales) mid-teens, combined low to mid-30s per restaurant per day.
    Answered directly
  • Chris Carril (for Chris O'Cull), KeyBanc: Margin outlook beyond Q3 and the narrowing pricing-inflation gap.
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: Gap was widest in H1 (pricing ~1% vs inflation low-to-mid 3%); Q3 inflation ~3% vs pricing mid 2%; from Q4 forward pricing and inflation expected to match, eliminating the dislocation.
    Answered directly
  • Zach Fadem (for Andrew Charles), TD Cowen: Why 350 openings per year versus the prior 400 ceiling, and what gates acceleration?
    Scott Boatwright, Chief Executive Officer: One restaurant per day is what the organization can support with ready-now leaders; above 350 risks opening quality. Incremental growth comes from company-owned Western Europe and partner markets; Alshaya is at 15 restaurants across three or four countries with meaningful acceleration planned for 2027.
    Answered directly
  • Drew North, Baird: Any regional World Cup benefit, and what the rolling pricing rollout showed?
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: About 300 restaurants within ~10 miles of stadiums saw a 50-100 bps comp benefit, but overall quarter impact was under five basis points. Rolling pricing measures resistance market by market; consumer reads allowed continued pushing, and the dislocation was a timing impact.
    Answered directly
  • David Palmer, Evercore ISI: Chipotle is doing and spending a lot for 1%-2% comps; how can you be confident momentum builds into 2027?
    Scott Boatwright, Chief Executive Officer: Q2 transaction growth reflects early days of Recipe for Growth and HEAP/throughput/hospitality investment; additional levers (menu innovation, rewards, brand communication, group occasions) show green shoots and will build through H2 2026 into 2027. Digital and brand ideas were described as industry-changing but not detailed.

Gemini 3.8 Flash7.0

Chipotle Mexican Grill reported Q2 2026 total revenue of $3.3 billion, an increase of 9.3% year-over-year, supported by comparable restaurant sales growth of 2.2% with a 1.0% transaction comp. Restaurant-level margin contracted 220 basis points to 25.2%, and adjusted diluted EPS was flat at $0.33. Backed by first-half execution, Adam Rymer, CFO, raised the full-year comp outlook to low single digits, while noting an industry-wide Cyclospora issue is creating a roughly 200 basis point drag entering Q3.

Key takeaways

  • Comparable restaurant sales grew 2.2% in Q2, driven by a 1.0% transaction comp and 1.6% pricing impact.
  • Restaurant-level margin declined 220 basis points to 25.2% due to input inflation running ahead of pricing, though margins are expected to realign in Q4.
  • An industry Cyclospora headwind emerged in late July, creating an estimated 200 basis point drag and lowering projected Q3 comps to approximately 1%.
  • Scott Boatwright, CEO, highlighted that more than 1,000 restaurants have received the High-Efficiency Equipment Package (HEAP), targeting 2,000 installations by year-end.
  • The board authorized an additional $1.3 billion in share repurchases, leaving total buyback capacity at $1.7 billion.

Key metrics

  • Total Revenue $3.3 billion up 9.3% year-over-year
  • Comparable Restaurant Sales Growth 2.2% including 1.0% transaction comp
  • Restaurant Level Margin 25.2% down 220 bps year-over-year
  • Adjusted Diluted EPS $0.33 flat year-over-year
  • Digital Sales Mix 38.3% up from 35.5% last year
  • New Restaurant Openings 101 units 100 company-owned, 80 Chipotlanes, 1 partner
  • Share Repurchases (Q2) $631 million average price of $32.55 per share
  • Active Rewards Members 23 million active member base

Financial highlights

  • Total revenue increased 9.3% year-over-year to $3.3 billion.
  • Adjusted diluted EPS was flat year-over-year at $0.33.
  • Digital sales reached $1.3 billion, representing 38.3% of total sales compared to 35.5% in the prior-year period.
  • Cost of sales rose 80 basis points to 29.7%, labor rose 30 basis points to 25.0%, and other operating costs rose 90 basis points to 14.9%.
  • Cash, restricted cash, and investments stood at $800 million following $631 million in Q2 share repurchases at an average price of $32.55 per share.

Guidance

  • Adam Rymer, CFO, raised full-year comparable restaurant sales guidance to the low single-digit range from about flat previously.
  • Adam Rymer, CFO, raised full-year menu pricing expectations to near the high end of the 1% to 2% range.
  • Adam Rymer, CFO, projects Q3 comparable sales growth of around plus 1%, assuming a roughly 200 basis point headwind from Cyclospora persists.
  • Scott Boatwright, CEO, reiterated full-year unit growth of approximately 350 restaurants, with about 80% featuring a Chipotlane.
  • Adam Rymer, CFO, expects Q3 cost of sales to be just under 30%, labor in the mid 25% range, and marketing in the low 3% range.

Q&A

  • Dennis Geiger, UBS: Can you provide more color on the cadence and trajectory behind the full-year low-single-digit comp guidance and the recent softening?
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: July maintained early momentum before decelerating by approximately 200 basis points in the second half due to an industry-wide Cyclospora issue, leading to a Q3 comp forecast of around plus 1%.
    answered directly
  • Danilo Gargiulo, Bernstein: Have you sized incremental CapEx and expected sales lift for the potential 1,000 to 1,500 store remodel program, and are you considering partners for international markets instead of owning?
    Scott Boatwright, Chief Executive Officer: Chipotle is testing store refreshes costing $100,000 to $300,000 per location with returns still undetermined, while international growth outside Western Europe will rely primarily on joint ventures and partner-operated models with groups like Alshaya, Alsea, and SPC.
    answered partially
  • Crystal Ivanko, J.P. Morgan: Should all labor efficiency from HEAP rollout be reinvested back into restaurant labor, and is it driving customer satisfaction and front-line traffic?
    Scott Boatwright, Chief Executive Officer: HEAP unlocks two to four hours of daily labor efficiency depending on restaurant volume, all of which is reinvested into prep completion and peak service line staffing.
    answered directly
  • Brian Mullan, Piper Sandler: What is working to drive in-restaurant loyalty participation, and do the 80% non-loyalty in-store transactions over-index to lower-income guests?
    Scott Boatwright, Chief Executive Officer: Non-loyalty in-store guests do not over-index to lower incomes, and Chipotle is launching an automated payment and enrollment process in August to reduce front-line friction.
    answered directly
  • Chris Carril, KeyBanc: What is the margin and pricing-versus-inflation outlook beyond Q3, and when does the dislocation between price and inflation narrow?
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: The gap narrows in Q3 with inflation near 3% against mid-2% pricing, and management expects pricing and inflation to fully align by Q4, removing the margin dislocation.
    answered directly
  • Jim Salera, Stephens: Can you share historical traffic lift benchmarks for LTOs, and do high-protein LTOs bring in new customers or simply drive frequency among existing guests?
    Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer: LTOs typically yield hundreds of basis points of sustained transaction lift, while Boatwright noted that protein LTOs drive both new customer acquisition and higher frequency.
    declined to quantify

Scott Boatwright provided unit cost ranges between $100,000 and $300,000 but stated it is too early to share expected sales lifts or financial returns.
  • Jim Salera, Stephens: Can you share historical traffic lift benchmarks for LTOs, and do high-protein LTOs bring in new customers or simply drive frequency among existing guests? Adam Rymer stated that specific historical lift figures for LTOs are not broken out or disclosed, providing only a generalized range of hundreds of basis points.

Notable quotes

  • “Opening a Chipotle nearly every day of the year is no small feat...” Adam Rymer, Chief Financial Officer

Claude Haiku 5.57.0

Chipotle's fiscal Q2 2026 revenue reached $3.3 billion, up 9.3% year-over-year, with comparable restaurant sales of +2.2%. Restaurant level margin fell 220 basis points to 25.2% and adjusted diluted EPS was flat at $0.33. HEAP restaurants do two or three more entrees in Max 15. Kinsale Capital Group, Inc. · fiscal Q2 2026 call, reported 2026-07-24 · 71 transcript paragraphs

Claude Opus 5.59.0

Kinsale reported Q2 2026 diluted operating EPS of $5.54, up 15.9% from $4.78, with a 75.5% combined ratio and 24.4% annualized operating ROE, while gross written premium fell 5% as the Commercial Property Division shrank. Excluding that division, GWP grew 3.7% in Q2 and 4.8% in 1H. Executives framed the quarter around holding profitability over growth in a softening E&S market and stepping up share buybacks.

Key takeaways

  • Michael Kehoe, Chairman, President and CEO, called commercial property a buyer's market where Kinsale is writing a shrinking volume of business.
  • GWP declined 5% overall but rose 3.7% excluding the Commercial Property Division, and new business submissions rose 6% (8% ex Commercial Property).
  • Stuart Winston, Chief Underwriting Officer, said London competition is creeping into long-tail occurrence lines and depressing Construction pricing.
  • The board added a $250M buyback authorization, bringing total capacity to $337M, which Kehoe described as the principal capital allocation while growth is limited.
  • Salmaan Allibhai, Chief Analytics and Technology Officer, said the accident year loss ratio could drift up over time as mix shifts away from property.

Key metrics

  • Diluted operating EPS $5.54 vs $4.78 Q2 2025, +15.9%
  • Annualized operating ROE 24.4%
  • Gross written premium -5% YoY; +3.7% ex Commercial Property Division
  • Combined ratio 75.5%
  • Expense ratio 21.7% vs 20.7%
  • Net investment income +19.9%
  • New business submissions +6% +8% ex Commercial Property
  • MS Amlin pricing index -5.9% vs -3.3% Q1 2026

Financial highlights

  • Diluted operating EPS was $5.54 versus $4.78 in Q2 2025, up 15.9%; net income rose 31.1% and net operating earnings rose 13.3%.
  • Combined ratio was 75.5%, including 4.5 points of favorable prior year development (vs 3.9) and 1.3 points of cat losses (vs under 1 point).
  • Expense ratio rose to 21.7% from 20.7%, while the other underwriting expense ratio fell to 10.3% from 10.6%.
  • Net written premium fell 1.4% while net earned premium rose 8.9%.
  • Net investment income rose 19.9%, float reached $3.4B versus $3.1B at end 2025, and new money yields are about 5.25% at a 4.25-year duration.

Guidance

  • Bryan Petrucelli, CFO, said the Q2 expense ratio of 21.7% is a good measure going forward, with a slight uptick in net commission possible after the June 1 reinsurance renewal.
  • Michael Kehoe, Chairman, President and CEO, expects modestly easier GWP comparisons in Q3-Q4 2026 since 60% of Commercial Property Division premium was written in 1H25.
  • Buyback capacity totals $337M after an additional $250M authorization, up from about $87M remaining.
  • Stuart Winston, Chief Underwriting Officer, said 5 new products are launching imminently with 10 more in the pipeline.

Q&A

  • Pablo Singzon, JPMorgan: What drove the accident year loss ratio improvement versus 2Q25, and will the ceding commission rate rise after the reinsurance renewal?
    Salmaan Allibhai, Chief Analytics and Technology Officer: Allibhai said losses came in below expectations amid normal variability and mix across a dozen statutory lines, with reserves as conservative as ever; Bryan Petrucelli, CFO, said treaties renewed June 1 so only one month was affected, a slight net commission uptick is possible partly offset by AI efficiency, and Q2's expense ratio is a good forward measure.
    Answered partially.
  • Dan Cohen, BMO: Is Q2 the trough in commercial property, and what drives the 3.7% ex-property growth, including any shift to the admitted market?
    Stuart Winston, Chief Underwriting Officer: Winston said there is no telling for 2027, citing pressure from London MGAs and traditional markets with large limits, said Kinsale will accept shrinking to meet return thresholds, noted London competition depressing Construction pricing, and said some flow to admitted is occurring but is not pervasive.
    Answered partially; no view on timing.
  • Andrew Andersen, Jefferies: Are inbound accounts rising from broker engagement or pricing, what are casualty loss trends and social inflation, and was the AY benefit all short-tail property?
    Stuart Winston, Chief Underwriting Officer: Winston said both, citing an idle broker initiative, with hit ratios roughly unchanged; Salmaan Allibhai put casualty loss trend at mid-single digits with little social inflation given small accounts and low limits, and short-tail lines performing especially well; Michael Kehoe added IBNR releases slower in long-tail occurrence lines.
    Answered directly.
  • Mark Hughes, Truist: How did property pricing move Q2 vs Q1, will the AY loss ratio drift up with mix shift, and what is buyback pacing?
    Michael Kehoe, Chairman, President and CEO: Kehoe said equilibrium will come eventually as cats occur but gave no timing; Stuart Winston said Agribusiness Property and small business property are stable and growing while Inland Marine sees more competition; Salmaan Allibhai said the AY loss ratio could drift up; Kehoe said the first $100M buyback was exhausted, about $80M remains of a $250M program plus a new $250M, with growth taking priority when it accelerates.
    Answered partially; declined to time the property cycle.
  • Hristian Getsov, Wells Fargo: How much are E&S casualty ROEs compressing, and how are broker commissions evolving in the soft market?
    Michael Kehoe, Chairman, President and CEO: Kehoe said all lines are managed to 20%+ ROE with most running ahead and 24.4% for the quarter, and that commission tension is normal but heightened in a soft market.
    Answered partially; no line-level ROE compression figure.

Unanswered

  • Hristian Getsov, Wells Fargo: How much are E&S casualty ROEs compressing as the segment softens? Michael Kehoe cited the 20%+ target and 24.4% quarterly ROE without quantifying compression by line.
  • Dan Cohen, BMO: How much have new products contributed to growth and what uplift is expected? Stuart Winston described a crawl, walk, run approach and gave no quantification.
  • Mark Hughes, Truist: When will property pricing reach equilibrium? Michael Kehoe said equilibrium will come as cats happen but had no insight on timing.

Notable quotes

Claude Sonnet 5.58.0

Kinsale reported Q2 2026 diluted operating EPS of $5.54, up 15.9% from $4.78, with annualized operating ROE of 24.4% and a combined ratio of 75.5%. Kinsale reported fiscal Q2 2026 diluted operating EPS of $5.54, up 15.9% from $4.78 a year earlier, with an annualized operating ROE of 24.4%. Gross written premium fell 5% year over year as competition in Commercial Property continued, while net earned premium rose 8.9%.   • Net earned premium up 8.9% year over year

Claude Fable 5.19.0

Deere reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5% year-over-year, with equipment operations net sales of $10.999 billion, up 6%, a 14.4% equipment operations margin, net income of $1.379 billion and diluted EPS of $5.10. Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, narrowed FY2026 net income guidance to $4.75 billion-$5 billion from $4.5 billion-$5 billion, while Deanna Kovar, President of Worldwide Agriculture & Turf, said model year 2027 early order program orders are up mid-single digits and reinforce the view that 2026 is the ag cycle bottom. Construction & Forestry grew 18% with 8 points of price, and Brent Norwood, CFO, flagged net tariff expense stepping up to around $1 billion in FY2027.

Key takeaways

  • Model year 2027 planter and sprayer early order program orders are up mid-single digits, with See & Spray factory adoption nearly doubling to about one-third of North American sprayers on order and more than 40% of planters taking the most advanced technologies, per Deanna Kovar, President of Worldwide Agriculture & Turf.
  • Deanna Kovar described the EOP results as reinforcing 2026 as the ag cycle bottom but said fundamentals support a measured recovery rather than a sharp rebound in 2027.
  • Direct tariff expense for FY2026 was lowered to approximately $1.1 billion from $1.2 billion as Section 232 tariffs dropped from roughly 25% to 15% effective 1 June, but Brent Norwood, CFO, expects FY2027 net tariff expense of right around $1 billion versus approximately $750 million net this year.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, said C&F has four to five months of orders on hand versus a typical two to three, driven by infrastructure, data center starts and rental channels, and PPA and C&F are each underproducing retail by a couple of percentage points in 2026.
  • Brent Norwood said Q4 PPA and C&F net sales should be similar to Q3, but margins will come in a little with no refund benefit and seasonally high R&D and SA&G in PPA and SAT.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion up 5% year-over-year
  • Equipment operations net sales $10.999 billion up 6% year-over-year
  • Equipment operations operating margin 14.4%
  • Diluted EPS $5.10
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion up 18%; price +8 points
  • Production & Precision Ag operating margin 13.2% down year-over-year on lower volumes and higher production costs
  • MY2027 EOP orders (planters and sprayers) up mid-single digits vs completion of last year's program
  • Used MY2023-2024 high horsepower tractor inventory down nearly 40% from a year ago

Financial highlights

  • Net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5%; equipment operations net sales of $10.999 billion, up 6%; equipment operations margin 14.4%; net income $1.379 billion; diluted EPS $5.10.
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales of $3.998 billion, down 6% (price +2.5 points, currency slightly over +1.5 points), with operating profit of $527 million and a 13.2% margin, down on lower volumes and higher production costs.
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales of $3.383 billion, up 12%, with operating profit of $622 million and an 18.4% margin, up year-over-year.
  • Construction & Forestry net sales of $3.618 billion, up 18% (price +8 points, currency roughly +0.5 point), with operating profit of $436 million and a 12.1% margin.
  • Financial Services net income of $219 million, up on favorable financing spreads; tariff refunds of $110 million recognized in Q3, bringing the FY2026 total to $382 million.

Guidance

  • Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, guided FY2026 net income to $4.75 billion-$5 billion, equipment operations cash flow to $5 billion-$5.5 billion, Financial Services net income to $870 million and the tax rate to 24%-26%.
  • Dan Pooley guided PPA net sales down approximately 10% with an 11%-12% margin, SAT up approximately 15% with a 14.5%-15.5% margin, and C&F up approximately 20% with 3 points of price and a 10.5%-11.5% margin.
  • Dan Pooley guided 2026 industry sales: US/Canada large ag down 15%-20%, US/Canada small ag & turf flat to up 5%, Europe approximately flat, South America down 15%-20%, US/Canada construction up 5%-10%, global forestry down 10% and global road building up approximately 10%.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, set FY2026 direct tariff expense at approximately $1.1 billion with no further refunds assumed; Brent Norwood, CFO, expects an FY2027 net tariff run rate of right around $1 billion.
  • Brent Norwood expects Q4 PPA and C&F net sales similar to Q3 with lower PPA and SAT margins on seasonal R&D and SA&G and no refunds.

  • Steve Fisher, UBS: With a half-year Section 232 benefit and no further refunds in Q4, is the roughly $800 million net tariff impact a headwind, tailwind or neutral into 2027?
    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Net tariffs will be a headwind next year: this year is about $1.1 billion direct less $382 million refunds for net exposure of approximately $750 million, while next year's run rate is expected right around $1 billion.
    Answered directly
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did something slip between 3Q and 4Q, why did tractor inventory build ahead of the industry, and what is the cadence exiting the year?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: He pushed back that Q3 changes should not be over-read; some demand was pulled ahead to manage Q4 risk, and Brent Norwood added PPA and C&F expect similar Q4 net sales to Q3 but margins will come in a little with no refund benefit and seasonal R&D and SA&G highs.
    Answered directly, with pushback on the premise
  • Chad Dillard, Bernstein: Why does C&F guidance imply roughly 50 bps price in Q4 versus +8% in Q3, and what are dealer rental fleet aspirations?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Full-year C&F price is back to 3% after moving 2.5% to 3% to 2.5%; Q3's 8% reflected a roughly 5% negative price comp from Q3 2025 incentives; 30%-35% of earthmoving transactions start as rental and dealer appetite to expand fleets could boost inventory fill next year.
    Answered directly
  • Jerry Revich, Wells Fargo: What regional variability did the EOP show, and could mid-single-digit growth become high single digits as programs wind down?
    Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division: U.S. trending slightly better than Canada; orders up mid-single digit and time will tell; See & Spray doubled on factory-installed sprayer orders and 40% of planters took the most advanced technologies.
    Answered partially; declined to project beyond mid-single digits
  • Steve Volkmann, Jefferies: How are C&F order programs shaping up and how much is dealer rental fleet loading?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: About four to five months of orders on hand versus a typical two to three, driven by infrastructure, data centers, the independent rental channel and dealer-owned fleets; 2026 underproduction gives an opportunity into 2027.
    Answered partially; rental fleet share not quantified
  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How could C&F technology mix and EOP precision take rates influence 2027 versus the historical 2-3 point mix benefit?

  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Could the 2028 European cap budget policy change pull demand into 2027, and any directional 2027 Europe forecast? Chris Seibert said too early to tell and unclear whether policy pulls demand forward or delays it.
  • Angel Castillo, Morgan Stanley: Does the backlog imply margins up, down or neutral next year? Chris Seibert spoke to measured inflationary pricing without a margin direction.
  • Steve Volkmann, Jefferies: How much of C&F orders is dealer rental fleet loading? Chris Seibert named rental as a driver but gave no share.

  • “At the same time, the underlying fundamentals continue to support a measured recovery rather than a sharp rebound in 2027.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division
  • “We have about four to five months of orders on hand, which is quite frankly, a little more than we would want to have.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations
  • “We are taking a measured approach there. We are committed to cover inflation here over time.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations
  • “I remain very confident in our team's ability to finish strong for the fiscal year.” Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer

  • Guidance: Equipment operations cash flow: $4.5 billion-$5.5 billion to $5 billion-$5.5 billion

  • Input and fertilizer cost uncertainty in Brazil and the U.S., with farmers seeking alternatives, per Deanna Kovar.

Claude Opus 5.58.0

Deere reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608B, up 5% YoY, with net income of $1.379B ($5.10 diluted EPS) and an equipment operations margin of 14.4%. Construction & Forestry sales rose 18% and Small Ag & Turf 12%, while Production & Precision Ag fell 6%; full-year net income guidance was raised to $4.75B-$5B. Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf, described model year 2027 early order program results as reinforcing the view that 2026 is the bottom of the ag cycle.

Key takeaways

  • Planter and sprayer early order programs are up mid-single digits versus the prior-year program, per Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf.
  • See & Spray factory adoption is nearly doubling to about one-third of North American sprayers on order, and over 40% of NA MY2027 planters carry advanced technology.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, said C&F has four to five months of orders on hand versus a typical two to three, driven by infrastructure, data centers and rental fleets.
  • Brent Norwood, CFO, said tariffs will be a headwind in FY2027, with a run rate near ~$1B versus ~$750M net in FY2026.
  • Chris Seibert said PPA and C&F are underproducing retail by a couple of percentage points each in 2026.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608B up 5% YoY
  • Net income / diluted EPS $1.379B / $5.10
  • Equipment ops operating margin 14.4%
  • PPA net sales $3.998B down 6% YoY
  • SAT net sales $3.383B up 12% YoY
  • C&F net sales $3.618B up 18% YoY; price +8 pts
  • Financial Services net income $219M higher YoY
  • MY2023-24 high-HP tractor used inventory down nearly 40% YoY

Financial highlights

  • Net sales and revenues were $12.608B, up 5%, with equipment operations net sales of $10.999B, up 6%.
  • PPA sales fell 6% to $3.998B with a 13.2% margin ($527M operating profit).
  • SAT sales rose 12% to $3.383B with an 18.4% margin ($622M).
  • C&F sales rose 18% to $3.618B on 8 points of price, with a 12.1% margin ($436M).
  • Tariff refunds of $110M were recognized in Q3, bringing FY26 to date to $382M.

Guidance

  • FY2026 net income attributable to Deere raised to $4.75B-$5B from $4.5B-$5.0B, and equipment operations cash flow to $5B-$5.5B from $4.5B-$5.5B, per Dan Pooley, Manager, Investor Communications.
  • PPA FY26 sales now down ~10% (bottom of prior 5%-10% range) with margin narrowed to 11%-12% from 11%-13%; SAT margin raised to 14.5%-15.5% from 13.5%-15%.
  • C&F FY26 sales up ~20% with price raised to 3 points from 2.5, and margin tightened to 10.5%-11.5% from 10%-12%.
  • Chris Seibert cut FY26 direct tariff expense excluding refunds to ~$1.1B from $1.2B, assuming no further refunds in Q4.
  • Brent Norwood expects Q4 PPA and C&F net sales similar to Q3, with PPA and SAT margins lower on seasonal R&D/SA&G and no refunds.

Q&A

  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: What drove the tariff estimate change to $1.1B, and how is the Deere-designed excavator launch going?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: The cut from $1.2B came mainly from the Section 232 rate on European imports falling from ~25% to 15% effective June 1, covering five of 12 months this year with further benefit in FY2027; three excavator models are in market with more over the next three to four years.
    Answered directly.
  • Steve Fisher, UBS: Will net tariff impact be a headwind, tailwind or neutral in 2027 versus 2026?
    Brent Norwood, CFO: A headwind: FY26 is ~$1.1B direct less $382M refunds, or ~$750M net, while the FY27 run rate is expected closer to ~$1B.
    Answered directly.
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did shipments slip from Q3 to Q4, and was tractor inventory built ahead of the industry?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: He pushed back, saying not to read much into Q3, as some demand was pulled ahead to manage Q4 risk and there is no inventory concern; NA tractor shipments started slowly and are catching up.
    Answered directly, disputing the premise.
  • Chad Dillard, Bernstein: Why does the C&F price guide imply low Q4 price after +8% in Q3, and what are dealer rental ambitions?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Q3's 8% partly reflects an easy comp against ~5% negative price in Q3 2025, the guide has moved 2.5%→3%→2.5%→3%, and 30%-35% of earthmoving transactions start as rental, with dealers wanting larger fleets that could boost inventory fill next year.
    Answered directly.
  • Angel Castillo, Morgan Stanley: What are the margin implications of EOP pricing for next year, and what does the FTC right-to-repair settlement mean for aftermarket targets?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Deere is taking measured inflationary price and is committed to covering inflation over time amid oil and supplier tariffs, but gave no margin direction; Brent Norwood, CFO, said the FTC agreement formalizes existing tools such as Operations Center PRO Service.
    Answered partially.
  • Rob Wertheimer, Melius Research: How are 8 Series tractor orders trending, and would Europe respond to crop price recovery like the U.S.?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: 8R orders are as expected with the MY2026 schedule closed, ~$5.05 corn is a good signal, Brazil is too early to call for 2027, Europe arable is challenged while dairy/livestock is stable, and there has been no recent step-up in order pace.
    Answered partially.

Unanswered

  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Will the 2028 EU CAP budget change pull forward or delay European demand, and what is the directional 2027 Europe view? Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, said it was too early to tell and gave no 2027 direction.
  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How will higher technology sales affect 2027 mix versus the historic 2-3 point benefit? Chris Seibert called it too early to tell, citing input, commodity and regional uncertainty, without quantifying.
  • Jerry Revich, Wells Fargo: Could mid-single-digit EOP growth end at high single digits? Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf, said only that time will tell.
  • Angel Castillo, Morgan Stanley: What is the margin implication of the EOP backlog for next year? Chris Seibert spoke to covering inflation over time but gave no margin direction.

Notable quotes

  • “Overall, we view the early order program results as an encouraging signal that reinforces our view that 2026 represents the bottom of the agricultural equipment cycle.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf
  • “At the same time, the underlying fundamentals continue to support a measured recovery rather than a sharp rebound in 2027.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf
  • “We have about four to five months of orders on hand, which is quite frankly, a little more than we would want to have.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations
  • “So there will be a bit of a step up in our tariff expense next year as we compare to this year.” Brent Norwood, CFO

What changed

  • Guidance: FY26 net income: $4.5B-$5.0B → $4.75B-$5B.
  • Guidance: Equipment ops cash flow: $4.5B-$5.5B → $5B-$5.5B.
  • Guidance: PPA net sales: down 5%-10% → down ~10%; PPA margin: 11%-13% → 11%-12%.
  • Guidance: SAT margin: 13.5%-15% → 14.5%-15.5%.
  • Guidance: C&F margin: 10%-12% → 10.5%-11.5%; C&F price: 2.5 pts → 3 pts.
  • Guidance: Financial Services net income: $860M → $870M.
  • Guidance: Europe ag industry: flat to up 5% → approximately flat.
  • Guidance: South America ag industry: down ~15% → down 15%-20%.
  • Guidance: US/Canada construction equipment industry: up ~5% → up 5%-10%.
  • Guidance: Global forestry: down 5% → down 10%.
  • Guidance: Direct tariff expense ex-refunds: ~$1.2B → ~$1.1B; net of refunds ~$900M → ~$750M.
  • New mentions: ExactEmerge, SmartGrade, Brazil move Agriculture Financing program, EU CAP policy 2028, FTC right-to-repair settlement
  • Dropped: US ag policy (RVO, E15), Precision Essentials renewal rates, Starlink JDLink Boost, CONEXPO 2026, $20B US manufacturing investment
  • Tone: Q2 framed 2026 as the ag bottom on early, hedged EOP reads, while Q3 cited completed EOP data as reinforcing that view yet explicitly framed 2027 as a measured recovery rather than a sharp rebound; tariff language shifted from refund tailwinds to a stated 2027 step-up in expense.

Risks mentioned

  • Tariffs remain a headwind, with an FY2027 run rate of ~$1B versus ~$750M net in FY2026, per Brent Norwood, CFO.
  • South America and EU softness, plus Black Sea disruptions, cloud 2027 visibility, per Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations.
  • Oil prices and supplier tariffs create a dynamic cost environment against pricing.
  • Elevated fertilizer and input costs are pressuring farmers, with impact varying by region, per Deanna Kovar.

Claude Haiku 5.57.0

Deere reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5% year-over-year, and net income attributable to Deere of $1.379 billion, or $5.10 per diluted share. Management narrowed or raised most FY2026 guidance items and lowered direct tariff expense to about $1.1 billion, while Production & Precision Ag sales are now guided down about 10%.

Key takeaways

  • Dan Pooley, Manager, Investor Communications, said equipment operations delivered a 14.4% operating margin.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, said Q3 shipments were pulled ahead "to kind of manage some risk here in Q4."
  • Brent Norwood, CFO, said net tariff expense will be a headwind in 2027, with a run rate closer to about $1 billion.
  • Chris Seibert said C&F has about four to five months of orders on hand, versus the typical two to three months.
  • Deanna Kovar, President of Worldwide Agriculture & Turf, said the programs are up mid-single digits and technology take rates were a pleasant surprise.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion up 5% year-over-year
  • Equipment operations operating margin 14.4% not stated
  • Net income attributable to Deere $1.379 billion, $5.10 per diluted share not stated
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales $3.998 billion down 6% year-over-year
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales $3.383 billion up 12% year-over-year
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion up 18% year-over-year
  • Engaged acres more than 520 million across nearly 1.2 million connected machines not stated
  • Monthly active digital users more than 450,000 nearly 440,000 in Q2 2026

Financial highlights

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion, up 5% year-over-year; equipment operations net sales $10.999 billion, up 6%.
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales $3.998 billion, down 6%; operating profit $527 million at a 13.2% margin.
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales $3.383 billion, up 12%; operating profit $622 million at an 18.4% margin.
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion, up 18%, with price up 8 points; operating profit $436 million at a 12.1% margin.
  • Q3 IEPA tariff refunds recognized were $110 million; Worldwide Financial Services net income was $219 million, higher year-over-year.

Guidance

  • Net income attributable to Deere FY2026: $4.75 billion to $5 billion, up from $4.5 billion to $5 billion in Q2 (Dan Pooley).
  • Financial Services net income FY2026: $870 million, up from $860 million in Q2 (Dan Pooley).
  • Equipment operations operating cash flow FY2026: $5 billion to $5.5 billion, up from $4.5 billion to $5.5 billion (Dan Pooley).
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales FY2026: down approximately 10%, versus down 5% to 10% in Q2 (Dan Pooley).
  • Small Ag & Turf operating margin FY2026: 14.5% to 15.5%, up from 13.5% to 15% (Dan Pooley).
  • Direct tariff expense excluding IEPA refunds FY2026: approximately $1.1 billion, down from $1.2 billion (Chris Seibert); total refunds recognized $382 million.
  • Brent Norwood, CFO, expects PPA and C&F Q4 net sales similar to Q3, and PPA and SAT Q4 margins lower on seasonal R&D and SA&G.

Q&A

  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: Is the $1.1 billion tariff expectation, about $100 million lower than originally anticipated, due to tariff relief or expected refunds? What is the status of the excavator launch?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: The FY2026 run rate fell from $1.2 billion to $1.1 billion, excluding refunds, mainly from Section 232 changes that cut the rate on imported goods from roughly 25% to 15%, affecting imports from Europe effective 1 June, so five of 12 months are affected; a FY2027 tailwind is expected. Brent Norwood said the first Deere-designed excavator models launched earlier this spring, with three models in market and shipments underway.
    Answered directly.
  • Steve Fisher, UBS: Is the net tariff impact about $800 million this year, and will it be a headwind or tailwind in 2027?
    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Net tariff expense will be a headwind in 2027. Deere pays about $1.1 billion in direct tariffs this year less $382 million of refunds, so net exposure is about $750 million; the 2027 run rate is expected closer to right around $1 billion.
    Answered directly.
  • Chad Dillard, Bernstein: What drives the implied C&F pricing for Q4 versus +8% in Q3, and how large does Deere want its dealer rental fleets to grow?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: C&F pricing was 2.5% at the start of the year, then 3%, then 2.5%, and is now back to the full-year guide. Q3's 8% benefited from easy comps, as Q3 2025 had about 5% negative price from incentives; Q4 has no outsized comps. Norwood said 30% to 35% of earthmoving transactions start as rental and dealer-owned rental fleets are growing.
    Answered directly, with figures.
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did timing slip between Q3 and Q4, and why did tractor inventory appear to build in Q3 ahead of the industry, given South America?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Seibert said shipment plans are set for the year and orders are on hand, though some demand was pulled ahead to manage Q4 risk. Deanna Kovar said North America entered 2027 slowly on tractor shipments. Norwood said PPA and C&F Q4 net sales should be similar to Q3, with no refund benefit in Q4 margins.
    Answered directly.
  • Angel Castillo, Morgan Stanley: What merchandising incentives and pricing trends are in EOPs, and what are the implications of the FTC settlement on right to repair and aftermarket business?
    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Chris Seibert said Deere is taking inflationary price with a measured approach and is committed to covering inflation over time. Norwood said Deere has always supported customers' ability to repair their own equipment, and the agreement formalizes existing tools such as Operations Center PRO Service.
    Partial; no pricing or aftermarket figures given.

Unanswered

  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How could mix contribute in 2027, given technology sales in C&F and early order take rates, versus the previously cited 2 to 3 point mix benefit? Seibert said it is too early to tell given input, commodity and shipment uncertainty, and that management is focused on controllables such as inventory management.
  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Could the EU capital budget policy change in 2028 pull demand into 2027 or delay European purchases, and what is the 2027 Europe view?   • “we believe the foundation is in place for a recovery, though its pace will ultimately depend on improving farm economics” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division
  • “We have about four to five months of orders on hand, which is quite frankly, a little more than we would want to have.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations
  • “Going into next year, we would expect a run rate that is going to be closer to right around $1 billion for the year.” Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer

  • Guidance: Financial Services net income FY2026: $860 million moved to $870 million.

  • Fertilizer and input cost pressure on Brazilian and U.S. farmers; larger farmers considering changes to contracted inputs (Deanna Kovar).
  • South America industry sales down 15% to 20% and U.S. and Canada large ag industry sales down 15% to 20% for FY2026.
  • EU capital budget policy change in 2028 could pull demand forward or delay purchases (Chris Seibert).

  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did anything slip between Q3 and Q4, given tractor inventory built in Q3, and what is the cadence exiting the year?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Seibert said not to read much into Q3, as shipment plans were set and some demand was pulled ahead. Brent Norwood, CFO, said Q4 PPA and C&F net sales should be similar to Q3, with no refund benefit and seasonally higher R&D and SA&G lowering PPA and SAT margins.
    Answered directly.
  • Chad Dillard, Bernstein: Why does C&F guidance imply about 50 bps of price in Q4 versus +8% in Q3, and how large should dealer rental grow?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Q3 had easy comps, as Q3 2025 had about 5% negative price from incentives, and Q4 has nothing outsized. Norwood said 30%-35% of earthmoving transactions start as rental, and dealer fleet expansion could lift inventory fill next year.
    Answered partially; no target fleet size or economics given.
  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: What drove the tariff cut to $1.1B, and how is the Deere-designed excavator launch going?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: The run rate moved from $1.2B to $1.1B excluding refunds, with five of 12 months benefiting this year and a further tailwind in 2027. Norwood said three Deere-designed excavator models launched this spring with very positive feedback, and more models follow over three to four years.
    Answered directly.
  • Jamie Cook, Truist Securities: How do production and retail compare by region for 2027, and what are 2027 pricing expectations?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: PPA and C&F are each underproducing retail by about a couple of percentage points in 2026. Deanna Kovar said EOP pricing was set months ago and the focus is covering inflation into 2027.
    Answered partially; no 2027 price figure.
  • Steve Volkmann, Jefferies: How are C&F order programs shaping up, and how much is dealer rental fleet loading?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: C&F has four to five months of orders on hand versus a typical two to three, driven by infrastructure, data center starts and rental channels.
    Answered partially; rental share not quantified.

Unanswered

  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Will the 2028 EU CAP policy change pull demand into 2027 or delay purchases, and what is the 2027 Europe view? Seibert said it is too early to tell and gave no directional view.
  • Angel Castillo, Morgan Stanley: What do EOP pricing trends imply for 2027 margins, and what does the FTC settlement mean for aftermarket parts? Seibert gave no incentive or margin direction, and Norwood did not address the $2-3B figure.
  • Jerry Revich, Wells Fargo: Could mid-single-digit EOP growth reach high single digits as programs close? Deanna Kovar said time will tell and pointed to technology take rates.

Notable quotes

  • “I would not read too much into the recent changes here in Q3.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations
  • “Demand trends in North America have remained relatively stable throughout the course of the year, albeit at very low levels” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division
  • “Things can turn quickly.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations

What changed

  • Guidance: Direct tariff expense: $1.2B to about $1.1B.
  • Guidance: Net income: $4.5B-$5B to $4.75B-$5B.
  • Guidance: PPA net sales: down 5%-10% to down about 10%; PPA margin: 11%-13% to 11%-12%.

  • New mentions: SmartGrade, ExactEmerge, FTC settlement
  • Dropped: Iran conflict, Harvest settings automation
  • Tone: Prior-quarter language centered on tariff refund and not extrapolating early order signals; this call adds mid-single-digit EOP growth and technology take rates, while Chris Seibert repeatedly said it is too early to tell on 2027 mix, Europe and pricing.

Risks mentioned

  • Softness in the EU and Brazil, with Europe arable challenged and dairy and livestock stable.
  • Net tariffs expected to be a headwind in 2027, with a run rate near $1B versus about $750M net this year.
  • Input cost and fertilizer uncertainty, with farmers seeking alternatives, per Deanna Kovar.
  • South America industry guided down 15%-20% for FY2026.

Gemini 3.6 FlashProduction6.0

Deere & Company reported net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion for the fiscal third quarter of 2026, representing a 5% increase year-over-year. Equipment operations generated an operating margin of 14.4%, with net income attributable to Deere & Company reaching $1.379 billion or $5.10 per diluted share. Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer, reiterated that fiscal 2026 represents the bottom of the agricultural equipment cycle.

Key takeaways

  • Model year 2027 early order program results are up mid-single digits compared to last year's completion, driven by advanced technology adoption.
  • Construction & Forestry order backlogs cover four to five months, supported by large infrastructure projects, data center buildouts, and dealer rental fleet expansion.
  • Full-year direct tariff expense expectations were lowered to $1.1 billion from $1.2 billion due to Section 232 duty reductions on imports from 25% to 15% effective June 1.
  • Dealer inventories for model-year 2023 and 2024 high horsepower tractors declined nearly 40% year-over-year.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion up 5% year-over-year
  • Equipment operations net sales $10.999 billion up 6% year-over-year
  • Net income attributable to Deere & Company $1.379 billion $5.10 per diluted share
  • Equipment operations operating margin 14.4% for the third quarter
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales $3.998 billion down 6% year-over-year
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales $3.383 billion up 12% year-over-year
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion up 18% year-over-year
  • Direct tariff expense guidance approximately $1.1 billion down from $1.2 billion prior expectation

Financial highlights

  • Equipment operations net sales rose 6% year-over-year to $10.999 billion in Q3 2026.
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales dropped 6% year-over-year to $3.998 billion, generating $527 million in operating profit at a 13.2% margin.
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales increased 12% year-over-year to $3.383 billion with an operating margin of 18.4% and $622 million in operating profit.
  • Construction & Forestry net sales surged 18% year-over-year to $3.618 billion, producing $436 million in operating profit at a 12.1% margin.
  • Deere recognized $110 million of incremental IEPA tariff refunds during Q3, bringing the total fiscal year 2026 refunds to $382 million.

Guidance

  • Dan Pooley, Manager, Investor Communications, raised full-year net income guidance to between $4.75 billion and $5.0 billion.
  • Dan Pooley, Manager, Investor Communications, projects equipment operations cash flows of $5.0 billion to $5.5 billion for FY 2026.
  • Production & Precision Ag full-year net sales are expected down approximately 10%, tracking to the bottom end of prior guidance, with operating margins at 11% to 12%.
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales are projected up approximately 15% for FY 2026, with operating margin expectations raised to 14.5% to 15.5%.
  • Construction & Forestry net sales are guided up approximately 20% for FY 2026, with operating margins narrowed to 10.5% to 11.5%.

Q&A

  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: What is driving the lower full-year tariff expectation of $1.1 billion, and what is the update on the excavator launch?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: The lowered direct tariff guidance to $1.1 billion stems from Section 232 tariff cuts from 25% to 15% effective June 1, while three Deere-designed excavators are in the market with positive customer feedback and more models planned over three to four years.
    answered directly
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did shipments or inventory slip between Q3 and Q4, and why did tractor inventory build ahead of the industry in Q3?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Q3 pulled ahead select demand to manage Q4 delivery risk while retail pace remained steady; Q4 margins will contract relative to Q3 due to non-repeating tariff refund benefits and seasonally higher R&D and SA&G expenses.
    answered directly
  • Steve Fisher, UBS: What will be the net tariff headwind or tailwind comparing FY 2026 to FY 2027?
    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Net tariffs present a year-over-year headwind in FY 2027, as FY 2026 net tariffs total $750 million ($1.1 billion direct costs less $382 million refunds) compared to an expected FY 2027 run rate near $1 billion.
    answered directly
  • Chad Dillard, Bernstein: What dynamics drove C&F Q3 pricing and Q4 expectations, and what is Deere's strategy around dealer rental fleet growth?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Q3 C&F pricing of 8% benefited from a negative 5% comparison in Q3 2025, while full-year pricing is guided to 3%, and rental transactions represent 30% to 35% of earthmoving activity.
    answered directly
  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How might product and technology mix influence performance in 2027?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Chris Seibert noted it is too early to quantify the 2027 mix impact due to ongoing market uncertainties in agriculture and regional dynamics in Europe and Brazil, despite positive technology adoption in early order programs.
    answered partially

Unanswered
